, for independent
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
.

•

Section
7.3
proves the trace moment bound using 2D interpolation of moments of the form
(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U+(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U
.

•

Section
7.4
obtains the differential inequality for the derivative of the interpolant.

•

Section
7.5
proves the trace inequality required for obtaining the differential inequality.

7.1.
Proving the subspace embedding guarantee for OSNAP

In this section we give the full proof of Theorem
3.2
.
See
3.2

Proof.

Let
X:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{pm}}SU
. We first assume that the collection of all the random variables

\{\xi_{(l,\gamma)},\mu_{(l,\gamma)}\}_{l\in[n],\gamma\in[s]}

in the unscaled OSNAP construction are fully independent, and later we will check what is the minimum independence needed.

We observe that to prove the theorem, it is enough to show that

\displaystyle\Pb\left(\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|\leq\varepsilon\right)\geq 1-\delta

because

\displaystyle\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|<\varepsilon

will imply

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon\leq v^{T}X^{T}Xv\leq 1+\varepsilon

and

\displaystyle(1-\varepsilon)^{2}\leq\|Xv\|^{2}\leq(1+\varepsilon)^{2}

for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and then the claim about singular values follows from the min-max principle. To show

\displaystyle\Pb\left(\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|\leq\varepsilon\right)\geq 1-\delta

we first use Markov’s inequality and obtain

\displaystyle\Pb\left(\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|^{2q}\geq\delta^{-1}\E[\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|^{2q}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\delta

Taking the
(2q)
th root, we have

\displaystyle\Pb\left(\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|\geq\delta^{-\frac{1}{2q}}\E[\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\delta

We observe that

\displaystyle\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|^{2q}=(s_{\max}(X^{T}X-I_{d}))^{2q}\leq\sum\limits_{i=1}^{d}(s_{i}(X^{T}X-I_{d}))^{2q}=d\tr(X^{T}X-I_{d})^{2q}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\Pb\left(\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|\geq\delta^{-\frac{1}{2q}}\E[d\tr(X^{T}X-I_{d})^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\delta

which, after simplification, becomes

\displaystyle\Pb\left(\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|\geq(d/\delta)^{\frac{1}{2q}}\E[\tr(X^{T}X-I_{d})^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\delta

For
q>\log(\frac{d}{\delta})
, we have

\displaystyle(d/\delta)^{\frac{1}{2q}}=\exp(\log(d/\delta)\frac{1}{2q})\leq\exp(\log(d/\delta)\frac{1}{2\log(\frac{d}{\delta})})\leq{\sqrt{e}}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\Pb\left(\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|\geq\sqrt{e}\E[\tr(X^{T}X-I_{d})^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\delta

By Theorem
7.3
, when

m\geq c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.1}\frac{d+q}{(\varepsilon/\sqrt{e})^{2}}
and
q\in\N
satisfying
2\leq q\leq m
and

\displaystyle pm\geq(\max\{\frac{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}q^{2}}{(\varepsilon/\sqrt{e})},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}q^{3}\})^{1+\frac{2}{q-2}}

we have

\displaystyle\E[\tr(X^{T}X-I_{d})^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}\leq(\varepsilon/\sqrt{e})

which will imply

\displaystyle\Pb\left(\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\delta

when combined with the previous calculations.
To ensure that the assumptions of Theorem
7.3
are satisfied, we need to choose
q
such that

\displaystyle pm\geq(\max\left\{\frac{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}\sqrt{e}q^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}q^{3}\right\})^{1+\frac{2}{q-2}}

Without loss of generality, we assume that
c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}>1
and
c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}>1
.
In addition, we recall that we also need the requirement
q>\log(\frac{d}{\delta})
for one of the previous steps.

Now, we claim that it suffices to require
m\geq 8c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.1}\frac{d+\log(d/\varepsilon\delta)}{(\varepsilon/\sqrt{e})^{2}}
, and

(7.1)

pm\geq\max\left\{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}{e}8^{2}e^{3},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}8^{3}e^{3}\right\}(\max\left\{\frac{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}{e}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{3}\right\})

and choose

\displaystyle q=\lceil 2\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})\rceil+2

to obtain the desired high probability bound. Since the quantity
\E[\tr(X^{T}X-I_{d})^{2q}]
remains unchanged if we only assume that subsets of the entries of
X
of size
2q
are independent instead of arbitrary subsets of the entries of
X
being independent and we choose
q=\lceil 2\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})\rceil+2
, we only need
S
to be an
O(\log(d/\varepsilon\delta))
-wise independent unscaled OSNAP.

Now we will check that this choice really works. First, by definition, we have
q>\log(d/\delta)
and the lower bound on
m
implies
q<m
, so we still have

\displaystyle\Pb\left(\|X^{T}X-I_{d}\|\geq\sqrt{e}\E[\tr(X^{T}X-I_{d})^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\delta

So it remains to check

\displaystyle pm\geq(\max\left\{\frac{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}{e}q^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}q^{3}\right\})^{\frac{q_{1}}{q_{1}-2}}

Note that
q=2\lceil\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})\rceil+2<8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})
because
d>10
, and therefore the above requirement is implied by

\displaystyle pm\geq(\max\left\{\frac{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}{e}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{3}\right\})^{1+\frac{2}{q-2}}

Since
q-2\geq 2\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})
, we also claim that

\displaystyle(\max\left\{\frac{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}{e}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{3}\right\})^{\frac{2}{q-2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\max\left\{\frac{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}{e}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{3}\right\})^{\frac{1}{\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})}}

will be bounded by an explicit constant, and this is where we need the extra factor
\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
inside the
\log
.
We observe that

\displaystyle(\frac{(\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{2}}{\varepsilon})^{\frac{1}{\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp(2\log\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})/\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))\cdot(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})^{\frac{1}{\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{2}\cdot(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})^{\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{3}

and

\displaystyle((\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{\frac{3}{\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp(3\log\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})/\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))\leq e^{3}

where we use the fact that
\log\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})>0
when
d>10
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle(\max\left\{\frac{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}{e}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{3}\right\})^{\frac{2}{q-2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\max\left\{\frac{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}{e}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{3}\right\})^{\frac{1}{\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta})}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}{e}8^{2}e^{3},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}8^{3}e^{3}\right\}

Therefore it is enough to require,

\displaystyle pm\geq\max\left\{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}{e}8^{2}e^{3},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}8^{3}e^{3}\right\}(\max\left\{\frac{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}{e}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}(8\log(\frac{d}{\varepsilon\delta}))^{3}\right\})

which is guaranteed by our assumption (
7.1
) on
pm
.

Moreover, recall that the definition of OSNAP requires
p\leq 1
. Therefore, if the requirement (
7.1
) already forces
p>1
, then we cannot construct an OSNAP satisfying the requirement. This will generally not be an issue because lower bound in the requirement (
7.1
) grows much slower than
m
. However, when
m
is small, this might happen because the constants can be large. For the completeness of the result, we mention that we can always use
p=1
even when (
7.1
) requires
p>1
. To prove this claim, it is enough to find values of
m
for which a dense
m\times n
matrix populated with random signs satisfies the OSE property for a given
0<\varepsilon,\delta<1
, and this follows directly for
m\geq c_{4}\cdot\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{\varepsilon^{2}}
using
[
38
, Theorem 4.6.1]
for some constant
c_{4}
.

∎

7.2.
Proving the decoupling lemma for OSNAP

Let
S
have the fully independent unscaled OSNAP distribution as described in Definition
3.1
. Then, recall that,

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{\gamma=1}^{pm}\xi_{l,\gamma}e_{\mu_{(l,\gamma)}}e_{l}^{T}

\displaystyle=:\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{\gamma=1}^{pm}Z_{l,\gamma}

where
\{\xi_{l,\gamma}\}_{l\in[n],\gamma\in[s]}
is a collection of independent Rademacher random variables,
\{\mu_{l,\gamma}\}_{l\in[n],\gamma\in[s]}
is a collection of independent random variables such that each
\mu_{l,\gamma}
is uniformly distributed in
[(m/s)(\gamma-1)+1:(m/s)\gamma]
and
e_{\mu_{(l,\gamma)}}
and
e_{l}
represent basis vectors in
\R^{m}
and
\R^{n}
respectively.

Recalling that our goal is to look at the moments of
(SU)^{T}(SU)-pmI_{d}
, we observe that,

\displaystyle U^{T}S^{T}SU-pm\cdot I_{d}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}U^{T}s_{i}s_{i}^{T}U\right)-pm\cdot I_{d}

where
s_{i}^{T}
denotes the
i\textsuperscript{th}
row of
S
. Then, letting
s_{ij}
denote the entries of
S
,

\displaystyle U^{T}S^{T}SU-pm\cdot I_{d}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}U^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{ij}e_{j}\right)\left(\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}s_{ij^{\prime}}e_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\right)U\right)-pm\cdot I_{d}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{ij}u_{j}\right)\left(\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\right)-pm\cdot I_{d}

where
u_{j}^{T}
denotes the
j\textsuperscript{th}
row of
U
. Separating the cases where
j=j^{\prime}
and
j\neq j^{\prime}
,

(7.2)

\displaystyle U^{T}S^{T}SU-pm\cdot I_{d}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{ij}^{2}u_{j}u_{j}^{T}-pm\cdot I_{d}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,j^{\prime}=1\\
j\neq j^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{n}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}s_{ij}^{2}\right)u_{j}u_{j}^{T}-pm\cdot I_{d}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,j^{\prime}=1\\
j\neq j^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{n}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}

Remark 7.1
.

In Equation (
7.2
), we decomposed the the embedding error
U^{T}S^{T}SU-pm\cdot I_{d}
into two parts, the diagonal term

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}s_{ij}^{2}\right)u_{j}u_{j}^{T}-pm\cdot I_{d}

and the off-diagonal term

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,j^{\prime}=1\\
j\neq j^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{n}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}

This decomposition is the key to understand why OSNAP model only needs
\tilde{O}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})
nonzero entries per column but the i.i.d. entries model might require
\tilde{O}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}})
nonzero entries per column. In fact, as we will see very soon, the key difference between the OSNAP model and the i.i.d. entries model is that the diagonal term vanishes in OSNAP model but does not vanish in the i.i.d. entries model.

By construction,
\sum_{i=1}^{m}s_{ij}^{2}=pm
, so the diagonal term becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}s_{ij}^{2}\right)u_{j}u_{j}^{T}-pm\cdot I_{d}=pm\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}u_{j}u_{j}^{T}-I_{d}\right)=0

and therefore we have

\displaystyle U^{T}S^{T}SU-pm\cdot I_{d}

\displaystyle=pm\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}u_{j}u_{j}^{T}-I_{d}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,j^{\prime}=1\\
j\neq j^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{n}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,j^{\prime}=1\\
j\neq j^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{n}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}

To analyze the off-diagonal term, we use the standard technique of decoupling,

Lemma 7.2

(Decoupling)
.

When
S
has the fully independent unscaled OSNAP distribution, we have

\displaystyle\E[\tr(U^{T}S^{T}SU-pm\cdot I_{d})^{2q}]

\displaystyle=\E\left[\tr\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1,j\neq j^{\prime}}^{n}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\right)^{2q}\right]

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\E[\tr(U^{T}S^{T}SU-pm\cdot I_{d})^{2q}]

\displaystyle\leq\E_{S,S^{\prime}}\left[\tr\left(2\left((SU)^{T}S^{\prime}U+(SU)^{T}S^{\prime}U\right)\right)^{2q}\right]

where
S^{\prime}
is an independent copy of
S
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{w}^{T}=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})
be a vector of independent random variables such that
\Pb(w_{i}=1)=\Pb(w_{i}=0)=1/2
. Then, whenever
j\neq j^{\prime}
,
\E[\mathbf{1}_{w_{j}\neq w_{j^{\prime}}}]=1/2
. So we have,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1,j\neq j^{\prime}}^{n}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}=2\E_{\mathbf{w}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{w_{j}\neq w_{j^{\prime}}}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\right]

By Jensen’s inequality
[
39
, Lemma 4.5, p.86]

\displaystyle\E\left[\tr\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1,j\neq j^{\prime}}^{n}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\right)^{2q}\right]

\displaystyle=\E_{S}\left[\tr\left(\E_{\mathbf{w}}\left[2\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{w_{j}\neq w_{j^{\prime}}}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\right]\right)^{2q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\E_{S}\E_{\mathbf{w}}\left[\tr\left(2\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{w_{j}\neq w_{j^{\prime}}}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\right)^{2q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\E_{\mathbf{w}}\E_{S}\left[\tr\left(2\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{w_{j}\neq w_{j^{\prime}}}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\right)^{2q}\right]

Fix
\mathbf{w}
, and let
J=\{j\in[n]|w_{j}=1\}
. Then,

\displaystyle\E_{S}\left[\tr\left(2\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{w_{j}\neq w_{j^{\prime}}}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\right)^{2q}\right]

\displaystyle=\E_{S}\left[\tr\left(2\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\sum_{j\in J,j^{\prime}\in J^{C}}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}+\sum_{j\in J^{C},j^{\prime}\in J}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\right)\right)^{2q}\right]

The key observation for decoupling is that the collection of random variables
\mathcal{S}_{J}:=\{s_{ij}\}_{i\in[m],j\in J}
is independent of the collection
\mathcal{S}_{J^{C}}:=\{s_{ij}\}_{i\in[m],j\in J^{C}}
, so the above expectation does not change if the collection
\mathcal{S}_{J}
is replaced by an independent copy
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{J}:=\{s^{\prime}_{ij}\}_{i\in[m],j\in J}
thought of as coming from the entries of an independent copy of
S
, say,
S^{\prime}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\E_{\mathcal{S}_{J},\mathcal{S}_{J^{C}}}\Biggl[\tr\Biggl(2\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Biggl(\sum_{j\in J,j^{\prime}\in J^{C}}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}+\sum_{j\in J^{C},j^{\prime}\in J}s_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\Biggr)\Biggr)^{2q}\Biggr]

\displaystyle=\E_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{J},\mathcal{S}_{J^{C}}}\Biggl[\tr\Biggl(2\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Biggl(\sum_{j\in J,j^{\prime}\in J^{C}}s^{\prime}_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}+\sum_{j\in J^{C},j^{\prime}\in J}s_{ij}s^{\prime}_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\Biggr)\Biggr)^{2q}\Biggr]

\displaystyle=\E_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{J},\mathcal{S}_{J^{C}}}\Biggl[\tr\Biggl(2\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Biggl(\sum_{j\in J,j^{\prime}\in J^{C}}s^{\prime}_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}+\sum_{j\in J^{C},j^{\prime}\in J}s_{ij}s^{\prime}_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}

\displaystyle\quad+\E_{\mathcal{S}_{J}}\Biggl[\sum_{j\in J,j^{\prime}\in J}s^{\prime}_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\Biggr]+\E_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{J^{C}},\mathcal{S}_{J}}\Biggl[\sum_{j\in J^{C},j^{\prime}\in[n]}s^{\prime}_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\quad+\E_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{J^{C}}}\Biggl[\sum_{j\in J^{C},j^{\prime}\in J^{C}}s_{ij}s^{\prime}_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\Biggr]+\E_{\mathcal{S}_{J},\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{J^{C}}}\Biggl[\sum_{j\in J,j^{\prime}\in[n]}s_{ij}s^{\prime}_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\Biggr]\Biggr)\Biggr)^{2q}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\E_{S,S^{\prime}}\Biggl[\tr\Biggl(2\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Biggl(s_{ij}s^{\prime}_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}+s^{\prime}_{ij}s_{ij^{\prime}}u_{j}u_{j^{\prime}}^{T}\Biggr)\Biggr)^{2q}\Biggr]

\displaystyle=\E_{S,S^{\prime}}\left[\tr\left(2\left((SU)^{T}S^{\prime}U+(S^{\prime}U)^{T}SU\right)\right)^{2q}\right]

where in the second equality the expectations we add are all
0
, and the inequality step follows by taking the expectations outside using Jensen’s inequality.
∎

7.3.
Controlling the trace moments of the embedding error for OSNAP

In this section we give the full proof of Lemma
7.3
.

Lemma 7.3

(Trace Moments of Embedding Error for OSNAP)
.

Let
S
be an
m\times n
matrix distributed according to the fully independent unscaled OSNAP distribution with parameter
p\leq 1
. Let
U
be an arbitrary
n\times d
deterministic matrix such that
U^{T}U=I
. Define
X=\frac{1}{\sqrt{pm}}SU
. Then, there exist constants
c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.1},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}>0
such that for
q\in\N
satisfying
2\leq q\leq m
, when
m\geq c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.1}\frac{d+q}{\varepsilon^{2}}
and
pm\geq(\max\{\frac{c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.2}q^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{\ref*{prop:momestdecoupmatrix}.3}q^{3}\})^{1+\frac{2}{q-2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\E[\tr(X^{T}X-I_{d})^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}\leq\varepsilon

Proof.

In Section
7.2
, we saw that when
S
has the unscaled OSNAP distribution

\displaystyle\E[\tr((SU)^{T}(SU)-pm\cdot I_{d})^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}\leq 2\E[\tr(\Gamma(S_{1},S_{2}))^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}

where
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are independent random matrices with the unscaled OSNAP distribution and
\Gamma(M_{1},M_{2})=(M_{1}U)^{T}(M_{2}U)+(M_{2}U)^{T}(M_{1}U)
. Let
\Gamma(t)
be as defined in Lemma
7.5
. Then, to show the statement of the theorem, it is enough to show that
\E[\tr(\Gamma(1))^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}\leq pm\varepsilon/2
. Now, by Lemma
6.9
, we know that,

\E[\tr(\Gamma(0))^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}=\E[\tr(\Gamma(G_{1},G_{2}))^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}\leq c_{\ref*{cor:indgaussianmom}}p\sqrt{\max\{d,q\}}\sqrt{\max\{m,q\}}

We want to find conditions for which
\E[\tr(\Gamma(0))^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}\leq pm\varepsilon/4
, for which it is enough to ensure
c_{\ref*{cor:indgaussianmom}}p\sqrt{\max\{d,q\}}\sqrt{\max\{m,q\}}\leq pm\varepsilon/4
. Clearly, this can only happen when
q\leq m
, and in this case the inequality holds when
m\geq\frac{c(d+q)}{\varepsilon^{2}}
. Thus, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(\E[\tr\Gamma(1)^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(0)^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}\leq\frac{1}{4}pm\varepsilon

By Lemma
7.5
, if
q
satisfies
q\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}]\leq

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{5}q)^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E[f(S_{1}(t),S_{2}(t))])^{1-\frac{k-2}{2q-2}}

We use different calculations for two different cases.

In the first case, we consider
pd<q
.

By convexity, the maximum must be attained on one of the two end points. Also, we observe that when
k=2q
, we have
\frac{k-2}{2q-2}=1
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}]\leq

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{5}q)^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{k-2}{2q-2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\{(c_{5}q)^{4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{q4-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{2}{2q-2}},

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{2q}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{q(2q)-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{2q-2}{2q-2}}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}\max\{(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{2}{2q-2}},

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{2q-4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2q^{2}-4q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{2q-2}{2q-2}}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}\max\{(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{2}{2q-2}},

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{2q-4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2q^{2}-4q}{q-1}}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}\max\{(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{1}{q-1}},

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{2q-4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2q^{2}-4q}{q-1}}\}

We will use Lemma 6.6 in
[
13
]
with
\alpha=\frac{1}{q-1}
. In this case, we have
{1-\alpha}={\frac{q-2}{q-1}}
, and
\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}=\frac{1}{q-2}
. Also, we want
K^{1-\alpha}=(c_{5}q)^{2q-4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2q(2q)-8q}{2q-2}}
, so we need
K=((c_{5}q)^{2q-4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2q^{2}-4q}{q-1}})^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}
.

By Lemma 6.6 in
[
13
]
, we have

\displaystyle(\E[\tr\Gamma(S_{1},S_{2})^{2q}])^{\alpha}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(G_{1},G_{2})^{2q}])^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}\cdot\frac{1}{q-1}+((c_{5}q)^{2q-4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2q^{2}-4q}{q-1}})^{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{q})^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}\cdot\frac{1}{q-1}+((c_{5}q)^{2q-4}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{q})^{\frac{2q^{2}-4q}{q-1}})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}q(q)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}\cdot\frac{1}{q-1}+(c_{5}q)^{2}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}q^{\frac{q}{q-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{6}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}q^{4}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle(\E[\tr\Gamma(S_{1},S_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{q-1}}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(G_{1},G_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{q-1}}\leq c_{6}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}q^{4}

By Taylor expansion, we have

\displaystyle(a+b)^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}\geq a^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}+b^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}

Using change of variable
c=a^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}+b^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}
and
d=a^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}
, we have

\displaystyle c^{\frac{q-1}{2q}}-d^{\frac{q-1}{2q}}\leq(c-d)^{\frac{q-1}{2q}}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle(\E[\tr\Gamma(S_{1},S_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(G_{1},G_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}\leq(c_{6}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}q^{4})^{\frac{q-1}{2q}}\leq c_{7}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}q^{2}

By previous analysis, the desired requirement
(\E[\tr\Gamma(S_{1},S_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(G_{1},G_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}\leq\frac{1}{4}pm\varepsilon
will be satisfied when

c_{7}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}q^{2}\leq pm\varepsilon

which means

\displaystyle pm\geq c_{7}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\frac{q^{2}}{\varepsilon}

In the second case, we consider
pd>q
. In this case, we have some
pd
factors in the bound for
\frac{d}{dt}\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}]
. We will eventually get rid of these
pd
factors by a replacing it by some powers of
\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}]
and some extra factors. To illustrate this idea, we start from following lemma to calculate
\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2}]
.

Lemma 7.4

(Second Moments)
.

Let
M
and
N
be
m\times d
independent random matrices such that the entries of
M
(and
N
) are uncorrelated and have variance
p
. Then,

•

\E[\tr M^{T}NM^{T}N]=p^{2}m
.

•

\E[\tr M^{T}NN^{T}M]=p^{2}md

Proof.

We have,

\displaystyle\E[\tr M^{T}NM^{T}N]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j,k,l}\E[m_{ji}n_{jk}m_{lk}n_{li}]

By uncorrelatedness of entries, the expectation is non-zero only when
j=l
and
k=i
. So,

\displaystyle\E[\tr M^{T}NM^{T}N]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\E[m_{ji}^{2}n_{ji}^{2}]

\displaystyle=p^{2}m

Similarly,

\displaystyle\E[\tr M^{T}NN^{T}M]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j,k,l}\E[m_{ji}n_{jk}n_{lk}m_{li}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j,k}\E[m_{ji}^{2}n_{jk}^{2}]

\displaystyle=p^{2}md

∎

By Lemma
7.4
, we have
\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2}]=2p^{2}md+2p^{2}m\geq 2p^{2}md
. By Hölder’s inequality,

\sqrt{2p^{2}md}\leq\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}}

In this case, we can replace the extra
pd
factors and obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}]\leq

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{5}q)^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2qk-4q}{2q-2}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{k-2}{2q-2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{5}q)^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{pd})^{\frac{2qk-4q}{2q-2}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{k-2}{2q-2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{5}q)^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}(2p^{2}md)^{1/4}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{1/4})^{\frac{2qk-4q}{2q-2}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{k-2}{2q-2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{5}q)^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}(\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}]^{\frac{1}{2q}})^{1/2}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{1/4})^{\frac{2qk-4q}{2q-2}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{k-2}{2q-2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{5}q)^{k}(pm)^{\frac{k-2}{q-1}}(\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}])^{\frac{k-2}{4(q-1)}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{qk-2q}{4(q-1)}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{k-2}{2(q-1)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{5}q)^{k}(pm)^{\frac{k-2}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{qk-2q}{4(q-1)}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{2(k-2)}{4(q-1)}+\frac{k-2}{4(q-1)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{5}q)^{k}(pm)^{\frac{k-2}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{qk-2q}{4(q-1)}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{(k-2)}{4(q-1)}}

If we want
1-\frac{(k-2)}{4(q-1)}=0
, then we need
(k-2)=4(q-1)
. Therefore, we can choose
k=4(q-1)+2=4q-2>2q
when
q>1
.

Using the convexity of the function
a^{x}b^{1-x}
in
x
as before, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}]\leq

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{5}q)^{k}(pm)^{\frac{k-2}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{qk-2q}{4(q-1)}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{(k-2)}{4(q-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq(4q-2)}(c_{5}q)^{k}(pm)^{\frac{k-2}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{qk-2q}{4(q-1)}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{(k-2)}{4(q-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\{(c_{5}q)^{4}(pm)^{\frac{4-2}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q4-2q}{4(q-1)}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{(4-2)}{4(q-1)}},

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4q-2}(pm)^{\frac{4q-2-2}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q(4q-2)-2q}{4(q-1)}}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q4-2q}{4(q-1)}}\max\{(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{(4-2)}{4(q-1)}},

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4q-2-4}(pm)^{\frac{4q-6}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q(4q-2)-2q}{4(q-1)}-\frac{q4-2q}{4(q-1)}}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}\max\{(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{1}{2(q-1)}},

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4q-6}(pm)^{\frac{4q-6}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q(4q-6)}{4(q-1)}}\}

by convexity.

At this point, we will use Lemma 6.6 in
[
13
]
again with
\alpha=\frac{1}{2(q-1)}
. Therefore, we have
1-\alpha=\frac{2q-3}{2(q-1)}
. Therefore, we have
\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}=\frac{1}{2q-3}
. So we need to use Lemma 6.6 in
[
13
]
with

\displaystyle K=((c_{5}q)^{4q-6}(pm)^{\frac{4q-6}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q(4q-6)}{4(q-1)}})^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}

in the notation there.

By Lemma 6.6 in
[
13
]
, we have

\displaystyle(\E[\tr\Gamma(S_{1},S_{2})^{2q}])^{\alpha}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(G_{1},G_{2})^{2q}])^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(c_{5}q)^{4}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}\frac{1}{2(q-1)}+((c_{5}q)^{4q-6}(pm)^{\frac{4q-6}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q(4q-6)}{4(q-1)}})^{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{10}q^{3}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}+((c_{5}q)^{4q-6}(pm)^{\frac{4q-6}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q(4q-6)}{4(q-1)}})^{\frac{1}{2q-3}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{10}q^{3}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}+(c_{5}q)^{2}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{2q}{4(q-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{10}q^{3}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}+(c_{5}q)^{2}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{10}q^{3}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}+c_{11}q^{2}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{12}q^{3}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}

In summary, we have

\displaystyle(\E[\tr\Gamma(S_{1},S_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2(q-1)}}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(G_{1},G_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2(q-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{12}q^{3}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle(\E[\tr\Gamma(S_{1},S_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(G_{1},G_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(c_{12}q^{3}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q-1}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}})^{\frac{2(q-1)}{(2q)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{13}q^{\frac{3(q-1)}{q}}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{13}q^{3}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{1}{2}}

where we use the fact that

\displaystyle(a+b)^{\frac{(2q)}{2(q-1)}}\geq a^{\frac{(2q)}{2(q-1)}}+b^{\frac{(2q)}{2(q-1)}}

by Taylor expansion.

We need
(\E[\tr\Gamma(S_{1},S_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(G_{1},G_{2})^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}\leq pm\varepsilon/4
, so the requirement is

\displaystyle c_{13}q^{3}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{pm\varepsilon}{4}

Since we have
m\geq\frac{c_{14}d}{\varepsilon^{2}}
for some constant
c_{14}
, we have
\varepsilon\geq\sqrt{\frac{c_{14}d}{m}}
, so it suffices to require

\displaystyle c_{13}q^{3}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q}}(\frac{d}{m})^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{pm\sqrt{\frac{c_{14}d}{m}}}{4}

which means

\displaystyle pm\geq c_{15}(pm)^{\frac{2}{q}}{q^{3}}

Combining the analysis for the two cases, it suffices to require

\displaystyle pm\geq(pm)^{\frac{2}{q}}\max\{\frac{c_{16}q^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{17}q^{3}\}

for some constants
c_{16}>0
and
c_{17}>0
.

This requirement is equivalent to

\displaystyle pm\geq(\max\{\frac{c_{16}q^{2}}{\varepsilon},c_{17}q^{3}\})^{\frac{1}{1-2/q}}

∎

7.4.
Differential inequality for the derivative of the interpolant

In this section we give the full proof of Lemma
7.5
.

Lemma 7.5

(Differential Inequality)
.

Let
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
be independent random matrices such that either both
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
have the fully independent unscaled OSNAP distribution with parameter
p
or both
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
have the unscaled OSE-IE distribution with parameter
p
. Let
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
be independent random matrices with i.i.d. Guassian entries each with variance
p
, and define the interpolated random matrices,

(7.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{1}(t)=\sqrt{t}S_{1}+\sqrt{1-t}G_{1}\\
S_{2}(t)=\sqrt{t}S_{2}+\sqrt{1-t}G_{2}\end{split}

Let
f(M_{1},M_{2})=\tr(((M_{1}U)^{T}(M_{2}U)+(M_{2}U)^{T}(M_{1}U))^{2q})
.
Then, there exists a constant
c_{\ref*{lem:diffineq}}
such that, for any
q\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\E[f(S_{1}(t),S_{2}(t))]\leq

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{\ref*{lem:diffineq}}q)^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E[f(S_{1}(t),S_{2}(t))])^{1-\frac{k-2}{2q-2}}

Remark 7.6
.

The proof of Lemma
7.5
relies on a technical trace inequality, Lemma
7.8
. To illustrate the main idea, we present the proof of Lemma
7.5
using Lemma
7.8
here first, and then prove Lemma
7.8
in the next section.

Proof of Lemma
7.5
.

For convenience, let
\Gamma(M_{1},M_{2})=(M_{1}U)^{T}(M_{2}U)+(M_{2}U)^{T}(M_{1}U)
and
\Gamma(t)=\Gamma(S_{1}(t),S_{2}(t))
.

Fix
M_{2}
and view
f_{1,M_{2}}(M_{1})=\tr(\Gamma(M_{1},M_{2})^{2q})
as a function of
M_{1}
. We shall first obtain an expression for
\frac{d}{dt_{1}}\E_{S_{1}(t_{1})}[f(S_{1}(t_{1}),M_{2})]
. For this purpose, we consider the construction from
[
13
]
. Recall that
S_{1}
can be written in the form
\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}Z_{(l,\gamma)}
where
\Xi=[n]\times[pm]
and
Z_{(l,\gamma)}=\xi_{(l,\gamma)}e_{\mu_{(l,\gamma)}}e_{l}^{\top}
(see definition
3.1
). We shall assume that the
Z_{(l,\gamma)}
are independent, with the additional remark that since all quantities involved are moments of order at most
4q
, the same calculations hold even if the
Z_{(l,\gamma)}
are
4q
-wise independent.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\pi\in\mathrm{P}([k])
be a partition of
[k]
. We construct the following family of random elements

\displaystyle\{Z_{(l,\gamma),j|\pi}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi,j\in[k]}

that is independent from the sigma-algebra
\sigma(S_{1},S_{2},G_{1},G_{2})

with the
following properties:

1.

Each
\{Z_{(l,\gamma),j|\pi}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}
has the same distribution as

\{Z_{(l,\gamma)}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}
.

2.

\{Z_{(l,\gamma),j|\pi}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}=\{Z_{(l,\gamma),j^{\prime}|\pi}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}
for indices
j,j^{\prime}

that belong to
the same element of
\pi
.

3.

\{Z_{(l,\gamma),j|\pi}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}
are independent for indices
j
that belong
to distinct elements of
\pi
.

More precisely, we construct these random elements in the following way. First, we choose a partition
\pi\in\mathrm{P}([k])
. Without loss of generality, consider the simple case where
\pi=\{A_{1},A_{2}\}
. Then we construct two i.i.d. copies of
\{Z_{(l,\gamma)}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}
, and call them
(Z_{(l,\gamma),A_{1}})_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}
and
(Z_{(l,\gamma),A_{2}})_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}
. For any
j\in A_{1}
, we just set
\{Z_{(l,\gamma),j|\pi}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}=\{Z_{(l,\gamma),A_{1}}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}
. Similarly, for any
j\in A_{2}
, we just set
\{Z_{(l,\gamma),j|\pi}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}=\{Z_{(l,\gamma),A_{2}}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}
.

In addition, we assume that the random elements

\displaystyle\{Z_{(l,\gamma),j|\pi}\}_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi,j\in[k]}

for different
k
and
\pi
are mutually independent from each other.

Using these random matrices, we can compute an expression for
\frac{d}{dt_{1}}\E_{S_{1}(t_{1})}[f(S_{1}(t_{1}),M_{2})]
using the following lemma,

Lemma 7.7

(Corollary 6.1,
[
13
]
)
.

For any polynomial
\phi:M_{m\times d}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\E[\phi(S_{1}(t))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{\infty}\frac{t^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{(k-1)!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{P}([k])}(-1)^{|\pi|-1}(|\pi|-1)!\,\E\Bigg[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma)1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma)k|\pi}}\phi(S_{1}(t))\Bigg],

where
\partial_{Z}\phi
denotes the directional derivative of

\phi
in the direction
Z\in M_{m\times d}(\mathbb{C})
.

We remark that Lemma
7.7
is proved in
[
13
]
for
S_{1}(t)
in the space of
d\times d
self-adjoint matrices with complex entries, but the result can be seen to hold for arbitrary matrices. Lemma
7.7
relies on the fact that the entries of
G_{1}
have the same mean and covariance as the entries of
S_{1}
for the vanishing of the terms corresponding to
k\leq 2
in the above expansion. Terms corresponding to
k=3
vanish due to our random variables being symmetric, so we start the series with
k=4
.

Proof of Lemma
7.7
.

Following the proof of Corollary 6.1 in
[
13
]
, we have

\frac{d}{dt}\E[\phi(X(t))]=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=3}^{\infty}\frac{t^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{(k-1)!}\times\mbox{}\\
\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(u_{j},v_{j})\in[m]\times[d]\\
j=1,\ldots,k\end{subarray}}\kappa((Z_{(l,\gamma)})_{u_{1}v_{1}},\ldots,(Z_{(l,\gamma)})_{u_{k}v_{k}})\,\E\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k}\phi}{\partial M_{u_{1}v_{1}}\cdots\partial M_{u_{k}v_{k}}}(X(t))\bigg],

Also, we observe that
\kappa((Z_{(l,\gamma)})_{u_{1}v_{1}},\ldots,(Z_{(l,\gamma)})_{u_{k}v_{k}})
is always
0
when
k=3
because the entries of
Z_{(l,\gamma)}
are symmetric.
Therefore, we conclude that

\frac{d}{dt}\E[\phi(X(t))]=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{\infty}\frac{t^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{(k-1)!}\times\mbox{}\\
\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(u_{j},v_{j})\in[m]\times[d]\\
j=1,\ldots,k\end{subarray}}\kappa((Z_{(l,\gamma)})_{u_{1}v_{1}},\ldots,(Z_{(l,\gamma)})_{u_{k}v_{k}})\,\E\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k}\phi}{\partial M_{u_{1}v_{1}}\cdots\partial M_{u_{k}v_{k}}}(X(t))\bigg],

Then the result follows by applying Lemma 4.1 in
[
13
]
.
∎

Using Lemma
7.7
with
\phi=f_{1,M_{2}}
and
t_{1}
as the variable name, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt_{1}}\E[f(S_{1}(t_{1}),M_{2})]=\frac{d}{dt_{1}}\E[f_{1,M_{2}}(S_{1}(t_{1}))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{2q}\frac{t^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{(k-1)!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{P}([k])}(-1)^{|\pi|-1}(|\pi|-1)!\,\E\Bigg[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{1,M_{2}}(S_{1}(t_{1}))\Bigg]

For clarity, we introduce the following notations. Let
X,Y
be two random elements taking values in measurable spaces
\mathscr{S}_{1}
and
\mathscr{S}_{2}
. Let
\phi:\mathscr{S}_{1}\times\mathscr{S}_{2}\to\R
be a measurable function. Assume that
\phi(X,Y)
is integrable. Define the function
\psi_{2}:\mathscr{S}_{2}\to\R
such that
\psi_{2}(y)=\E\phi(X,y)
. Then the notation
\E_{X}(\phi(X,Y))
stands for
\psi_{2}(Y)
. Similarly, we define the function
\psi_{1}:\mathscr{S}_{1}\to\R
such that
\psi_{1}(x)=\E\phi(x,Y)
and the notation
\E_{Y}(\phi(X,Y))
stands for the random variable
\psi_{1}(X)
. With these notation and observing that we can plug the random variable
S_{2}(t_{2})
into
M_{2}
in the previous identity (since the identity holds for arbitrary
M_{2}
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt_{1}}\E_{S_{1}(t_{1})}[f(S_{1}(t_{1}),S_{2}(t_{2}))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{2q}\frac{t^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{(k-1)!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{P}([k])}(-1)^{|\pi|-1}(|\pi|-1)!\,

\displaystyle\cdot\E_{S_{1}(t_{1})}\Bigg[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}(S_{1}(t_{1}))\Bigg]

By differentiation under integral sign
[
40
, Theorem 2.27]
and iterated integration, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt_{1}}\E_{S_{2}(t_{2})}\E_{S_{1}(t_{1})}[f(S_{1}(t_{1}),S_{2}(t_{2}))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{S_{2}(t_{2})}\frac{d}{dt_{1}}\E_{S_{1}(t_{1})}[f(S_{1}(t_{1}),S_{2}(t_{2}))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{2q}\frac{t^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{(k-1)!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{P}([k])}(-1)^{|\pi|-1}(|\pi|-1)!\,

\displaystyle\E_{S_{2}(t_{2})}\E_{S_{1}(t_{1})}\Bigg[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}(S_{1}(t_{1}))\Bigg],

By the independence between
S_{1}(t_{1})
and
S_{2}(t_{2})
, we can use iterated expectation and conclude that

\displaystyle\E_{S_{2}(t_{2})}\E_{S_{1}(t_{1})}[f(S_{1}(t_{1}),S_{2}(t_{2}))]=\E[f(S_{1}(t_{1}),S_{2}(t_{2}))]

and

\displaystyle\E_{S_{2}(t_{2})}\E_{S_{1}(t_{1})}\Bigg[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}(S_{1}(t_{1}))\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\Bigg[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}(S_{1}(t_{1}))\Bigg]

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt_{1}}\E[f(S_{1}(t_{1}),S_{2}(t_{2}))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{2q}\frac{t^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{(k-1)!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{P}([k])}(-1)^{|\pi|-1}(|\pi|-1)!\,

\displaystyle\E\Bigg[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}(S_{1}(t_{1}))\Bigg],

Similarly, we can define
f_{2,M_{1}}(M_{2})=\tr(\Gamma(M_{1},M_{2})^{2q})
and obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt_{2}}\E[f(S_{1}(t_{1}),S_{2}(t_{2}))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{2q}\frac{t^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{(k-1)!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{P}([k])}(-1)^{|\pi|-1}(|\pi|-1)!\,

\displaystyle\E\Bigg[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{2,S_{1}(t_{1})}(S_{2}(t_{2}))\Bigg],

We decompose

\displaystyle\phi(t)=\frac{d}{dt}\E[f(S_{1}(t),S_{2}(t))]

as
\phi(t)=\psi_{1}\circ\psi_{2}(t)
where

\psi_{1}(t_{1},t_{2})=\E[f(S_{1}(t_{1}),S_{2}(t_{2}))]

and
(t_{1},t_{2})=\psi_{2}(t)=(t,t)
.

By chain rule, we have

(7.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{d}{dt}\E[f(S_{1}(t),S_{2}(t))]\\
=&\Bigg[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{d}{dt_{1}}(\E[f(S_{1}(t),S_{2}(t))])&\frac{d}{dt_{2}}(\E[f(S_{1}(t),S_{2}(t))])\end{array}\Bigg]\cdot\Bigg[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{d}{dt_{1}}(t_{1})\\
\frac{d}{dt_{2}}(t_{2})\end{array}\Bigg]\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{2q}\frac{t^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{(k-1)!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{P}([k])}(-1)^{|\pi|-1}(|\pi|-1)!\,\\
&\E\Bigg[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{1,S_{2}(t)}(S_{1}(t))\Bigg]\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{2q}\frac{t^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{(k-1)!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{P}([k])}(-1)^{|\pi|-1}(|\pi|-1)!\,\\
&\E\Bigg[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{2,S_{1}(t)}(S_{2}(t))\Bigg]\\
=:&T_{1}+T_{2}\end{split}

where
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
denote the first and second sum respectively.

The next step is to bound
\frac{d}{dt}\E[f(S_{1}(t),S_{2}(t))]
.

From Equation (
7.4
), we notice that the terms inside the expectation in both
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
are directional derivatives of
f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}
and
f_{2,S_{1}(t_{1})}
along
Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi},\ldots,Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}
. Using a general expression for derivatives of multinomials using product rule, we have, for any deterministic
m\times d
matrices
B_{1},\ldots,B_{k},M_{1}
and
M_{2}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{B_{1}}\cdots\partial_{B_{k}}f_{1,M_{2}}(M_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\sym(k)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{1},\ldots,r_{k+1}\geq 0\\
r_{1}+\cdots+r_{k+1}=2q-k\end{subarray}}\tr(\Gamma(M_{1},M_{2})^{r_{1}}((B_{\sigma(1)}U)^{T}M_{2}U+(M_{2}U)^{T}B_{\sigma(1)}U)\Gamma(M_{1},M_{2})^{r_{2}}

\displaystyle((B_{\sigma(2)}U)^{T}M_{2}U+(M_{2}U)^{T}B_{\sigma(2)}U)\cdots\Gamma(M_{1},M_{2})^{r_{k}}

\displaystyle((B_{\sigma(k)}U)^{T}M_{2}U+(M_{2}U)^{T}B_{\sigma(k)}U)\Gamma(M_{1},M_{2})^{r_{k+1}})

In our case, for each fixed
(l,\gamma)
, we have to analyse (in the case of
T_{1}
)
\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}\allowbreak f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}(S_{1}(t_{1}))
, which means we have
B_{\lambda}=Z_{(l,\gamma),\lambda|\pi}
for
\lambda\in[k]
, and
M_{2}=S_{2}(t)
. So terms of the form
(B_{\lambda}U)^{T}M_{2}U
become,

\displaystyle(Z_{(l,\gamma),\lambda|\pi}U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U\allowbreak=\xi_{(l,\gamma),\lambda|\pi}\mathbf{u}_{l}e_{\mu_{(l,\gamma),\lambda|\pi}}^{T}S_{2}(t)U

where
\mathbf{u}_{l}
is the column vector such that
\mathbf{u}_{l}^{T}
is the
l
th row of
U
and we use the fact that
Z_{(l,\gamma)}=\xi_{(l,\gamma)}e_{\mu_{(l,\gamma)}}e_{l}^{T}
with
\mu_{(l,\gamma)}
being a uniformly distributed random variable on
[(m/s)(\gamma-1)+1:(m/s)\gamma]
and
\xi_{(l,\gamma)}
being a random sign.

Let
\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,1}=\xi_{(l,\gamma)}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{T}
and
\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,2}=e_{\mu_{(l,\gamma),\lambda|\pi}}(S_{2}(t)U)
. Then,

\displaystyle(Z_{(l,\gamma),\lambda|\pi}U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U\allowbreak=\xi_{(l,\gamma)}\mathbf{u}_{l}e_{\mu_{(l,\gamma),\lambda|\pi}}^{T}S_{2}(t)U=\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,1}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,2}

Thus, terms of the form
(B_{\lambda}U)^{T}M_{2}U+(M_{2}U)^{T}B_{\lambda}U
become

\displaystyle(Z_{(l,\gamma),\lambda|\pi}U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U+(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),\lambda|\pi}U

\displaystyle=\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,1}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,2}+\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,2}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau\in\sym(\{1,2\})}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,\tau(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,\tau(2)}

Doing this for all terms

\displaystyle(B_{\sigma(1)}U)^{T}M_{2}U+(M_{2}U)^{T}B_{\sigma(1)}U,\ldots,(B_{\sigma(1)}U)^{T}M_{2}U+(M_{2}U)^{T}B_{\sigma(1)}U

in the expansion of
\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}\allowbreak f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}(S_{1}(t_{1}))
(for a fixed
(l,\gamma)
and
\pi\in P([k])
), we get

\displaystyle\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}(S_{1}(t_{1}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in\sym([k])}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{1},\ldots,r_{k+1}\geq 0\\
r_{1}+\cdots+r_{k+1}=2q-k\end{subarray}}\sum_{\tau_{1},...,\tau_{k}\in\sym(\{1,2\})}\tr\Gamma(t)^{r_{1}}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(1),\tau_{1}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(1),\tau_{1}(2)}

\displaystyle\cdot\Gamma(t)^{r_{2}}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(2),\tau_{2}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(2),\tau_{2}(2)}\cdots\Gamma(t)^{r_{k}}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(k),\tau_{k}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(k),\tau_{k}(2)}\Gamma(t)^{r_{k+1}}

Fixing
k
and
\pi
, summing over
(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]
, and taking expectations, we have

\displaystyle\E\left[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}(S_{1}(t_{1}))\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\sym([k])}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{1},\ldots,r_{k+1}\geq 0\\
r_{1}+\cdots+r_{k+1}=2q-k\end{subarray}}\sum_{\tau_{1},...,\tau_{k}\in\sym(\{1,2\})}\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}

\displaystyle\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{r_{1}}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(1),\tau_{1}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(1),\tau_{1}(2)}\cdot\Gamma(t)^{r_{2}}

\displaystyle\cdots\Gamma(t)^{r_{k}}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(k),\tau_{k}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(k),\tau_{k}(2)}\Gamma(t)^{r_{k+1}}]

By Lemma
7.8
(when
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
have the unscaled OSNAP distribution) and by Lemma
9.5
(when
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
have the unscaled OSE-IE distribution) with
\Upsilon_{1}=\Gamma(t)^{r_{2}},...,\Upsilon_{k}=\Gamma(t)^{r_{k}+r_{1}}
, we have

(7.5)

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{r_{1}}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(1),\tau_{1}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(1),\tau_{1}(2)}\cdot\Gamma(t)^{r_{2}}

\displaystyle\cdots\Gamma(t)^{r_{k}}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(k),\tau_{k}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(k),\tau_{k}(2)}\Gamma(t)^{r_{k+1}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\E[\tr\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(1),\tau_{1}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(1),\tau_{1}(2)}\cdot\Gamma(t)^{r_{2}}

\displaystyle\cdots\Gamma(t)^{r_{k}}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(k),\tau_{k}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\sigma(k),\tau_{k}(2)}\Gamma(t)^{r_{k+1}}\Gamma(t)^{r_{1}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\E[\tr\Theta_{(l,\gamma),1,\tau_{1}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),1,\tau_{1}(2)}\Upsilon_{1}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),2,\tau_{2}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),2,\tau_{2}(2)}

\displaystyle\cdots\Upsilon_{2}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),k,\tau_{k}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),k,\tau_{k}(2)}\Upsilon_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(c_{\ref*{lem:decouptraceineq}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{\frac{1}{q}\cdot\frac{2q-k}{2q-2}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{k}{2q}}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\E\left[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}(S_{1}(t_{1}))\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle k!\binom{2q}{k}2^{k}(c_{\ref*{lem:decouptraceineq}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{\frac{1}{q}\cdot\frac{2q-k}{2q-2}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{k}{2q}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(4q)^{k}(c_{\ref*{lem:decouptraceineq}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{1}{2q}(k-2/r)}

where
r=\frac{2q-2}{2q-k}
.

Going back to the expression for
T_{1}
and using
[
13
, Lemma 6.4]
, we get,

\displaystyle T_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{2q}\frac{t^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{(k-1)!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{P}([k])}(-1)^{|\pi|-1}(|\pi|-1)!

\displaystyle\cdot\E\Bigg[\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1|\pi}}\cdots\partial_{Z_{(l,\gamma),k|\pi}}f_{1,S_{2}(t_{2})}(S_{1}(t_{1}))\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{2q}\frac{1}{(k-1)!}2^{k}(k-1)!

\displaystyle\cdot(4q)^{k}(c_{\ref*{lem:decouptraceineq}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{1}{2q}(k-2/r)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{2q}(8q)^{k}c_{1}^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{1}{2q}(k-2/r)}

We similarly get
T_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=4}^{2q}(8q)^{k}c_{1}^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{1}{2q}(k-2/r)}
.

Going back, we have,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}]\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=4}^{2q}(8c_{1}q)^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{1}{2q}(k-2/r)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=4}^{2q}2^{-k}(16c_{1}q)^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{1}{2q}(k-2/r)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(16c_{1}q)^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{1-\frac{1}{2q}(k-2/r)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\limits_{4\leq k\leq 2q}(c_{5}q)^{k}((pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{qk-2q}{q-1}}(\E[f(S_{1}(t),S_{2}(t))])^{1-\frac{k-2}{2q-2}}

∎

7.5.
Proving the trace inequality needed to obtain the differential inequality for the derivative of the interpolant

In this section, we explain the following trace inequality result which is the key to prove the bound (
7.5
) in the proof of Lemma
7.5
.

Lemma 7.8

(Trace Inequalities for OSNAP)
.

Let
S_{1}(t)
and
S_{2}(t)
be as in Lemma
7.5
with both having the fully independent unscaled OSNAP distribution. Let

\displaystyle\Gamma(t)=(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)+(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{1}(t)U)

Let
\mathcal{Z}=\{\xi_{(l,\gamma)}:(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]\}\cup\{\mu_{(l,\gamma)}:(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]\}
be the family of mutually independent random variables generating an instance of
S_{1}
with the fully independent unscaled OSNAP distribution. Let
q\geq 2
and
3\leq k\leq 2q
. Let
\{\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in[k]}
be a family of (possibly dependent) random elements, where for each
\lambda\in[k]
, the random element

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}=\{\xi_{(l,\gamma),\lambda}:(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]\}\cup\{\mu_{(l,\gamma),\lambda}:(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]\}

has the same distribution as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}=\{\xi_{(l,\gamma)}:(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]\}\cup\{\mu_{(l,\gamma)}:(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]\}

Let
Z_{(l,\gamma)}=\xi_{(l,\gamma)}e_{\mu_{(l,\gamma)}}e_{l}^{T}
and
Z_{(l,\gamma),\lambda}=\xi_{(l,\gamma),\lambda}e_{\mu_{(l,\gamma),\lambda}}e_{l}^{T}
.
Let
\{\Upsilon_{1},...,\Upsilon_{k}\}
be a family of
L_{\infty}(S_{\infty}^{d})
random matrices.
Assume further that the collection
\{\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in[k]}
is independent of
S_{1},S_{2},G_{1},G_{2}
, and
\{\Upsilon_{1},...,\Upsilon_{k}\}
. (In other words,
\{\Upsilon_{1},...,\Upsilon_{k}\}
can possibly be dependent with
S_{1},S_{2},G_{1},G_{2}
.)
For each
(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]
and
\lambda\in k
, we define random vectors
\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,1},\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,2}\in\R^{d}
such that

\displaystyle\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,1}=\xi_{(l,\gamma),\lambda}u_{l}^{T}\text{ and }\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,2}=e_{\mu_{(l,\gamma),\lambda}}^{T}S_{2}(t)U

where
e_{\mu_{(l,\gamma),\lambda}}
represents the
\mu_{(l,\gamma),\lambda}
th coordinate vector. Then, given
0\leq\beta_{1},...,\beta_{k}\leq+\infty
such that
\sum\limits_{\lambda=1}^{k}\frac{1}{\beta_{\lambda}}=1-\frac{k}{2q}
,
\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{k}\in\sym(\{1,2\})
, there exists
c_{\ref*{lem:decouptraceineq}}>0
such that

(7.6)

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\E[\tr\Theta_{(l,\gamma),1,\tau_{1}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),1,\tau_{1}(2)}\Upsilon_{1}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),2,\tau_{2}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),2,\tau_{2}(2)}

\displaystyle\cdots\Upsilon_{2}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),k,\tau_{k}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),k,\tau_{k}(2)}\Upsilon_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(c_{\ref*{lem:decouptraceineq}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2qk-4q}{2q-2}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{\frac{1}{q}\cdot\frac{2q-k}{2q-2}}\prod\limits_{\lambda=1}^{k}\lVert\Upsilon_{\lambda}\rVert_{\beta_{\lambda}}

Remark 7.9
.

The main idea of this lemma is simple. We first use matrix Holder inequality to transform the left hand side into smaller factors, and then we bound those small factors separately. Following this idea, there could be different variants of this lemma. However, not all of them will eventually lead to the optimal dependency of the sparsity on
\varepsilon
. We will explain the idea on how to choose the correct bound. As explained in Proposition
7.3
, the sparsity requirement comes from the condition

\displaystyle(\E[\tr\Gamma(1)^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(0)^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}\leq\frac{1}{4}pm\varepsilon

If we want this condition to be implied by a requirement of the type

\displaystyle pm>\frac{C(\log(d))^{\text{(some power)}}}{\varepsilon}

then a natural attempt would be to try to bound
(\E[\tr\Gamma(1)^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(0)^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}
by just a constant times some power of
\log(d)
.

To bound
(\E[\tr\Gamma(1)^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(0)^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}
, we combine our differential inequality with Lemma 6.6 in
[
13
]
.

By Lemma 6.6 in
[
13
]
, we can get the bound of the type

\displaystyle(\E[\tr\Gamma(1)^{2q}])^{\alpha}-(\E[\tr\Gamma(0)^{2q}])^{\alpha}\leq C\alpha+K^{1-\alpha}

if we have a differential inequality of the form

\displaystyle|\frac{d}{dt}(\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}])|\leq C\max\{(\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}])^{1-\alpha},K^{1-\alpha}\}

So we mainly want to obtain a differential inequality where
C
and
K
only contain factors of
\log(d)
but do not contain any positive powers of
pm
or any negative power of
\varepsilon\approx\sqrt{d/m}
. To this end, we need to choose a variant of the bound for the left hand side of (
7.6
) which does not contain any positive powers of
pm
or any negative power of
\varepsilon\approx\sqrt{d/m}
.

Therefore, when choosing the different variants of the bound in Lemma
7.8
, we seek to remove those factors that we do not want. One natural attempt would be to use the method similar to the proof of the second inequality in Theorem 2.9 in
[
13
]
, where they first bound the small factors that arise after using Holder by some matrix parameters, and then replace those matrix parameters by the trace moments by Jensen inequality. In our case, this means to replace
\sqrt{pmpd}
by
(\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}
, thereby removing the factors
pm
and
pd
.

However, in our case, after bounding the small factors obtained by directly separating the left hand side of (
7.6
) using Holder, we can only obtain factors of the form
\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}}
and
\sqrt{\max\{pm,q\}}
. (Recall that
q\sim\log(d/\varepsilon\delta)
). The product
\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}\cdot\max\{pm,q\}}
of these two factors can be interchanged by the factor
\sqrt{pmpd}
only when
pd\geq\log(d/\varepsilon\delta)
. But this already means that
pm\geq O(\frac{\log(d/\varepsilon\delta)}{\varepsilon^{2}})
, since
\varepsilon=O(\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}})
.

To get rid of the unbalanced factor
\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}\cdot\max\{pm,q\}}
, we develop a completely different method. We combine several factors at early stage and then use a key observation, Lemma
7.11
, to replace this combined factor by
(\E[\tr\Gamma(t)^{2q}])^{\frac{1}{2q}}
directly. Eventually this method allowed us to get a more precise upper estimation, namely the right hand side of (
7.6
). In this upper estimation, although there are still some factors of
\max\{pd,q\}
, they are not harmful, because
\max\{pd,q\}
is of smaller order than
\sqrt{pdpm}
(see the proof of Proposition
7.3
for details).

Proof of Lemma
7.8
.

Let

\displaystyle F(\Upsilon_{1},...,\Upsilon_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\E[\tr\Theta_{(l,\gamma),1,\tau_{1}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),1,\tau_{1}(2)}\Upsilon_{1}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),2,\tau_{2}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),2,\tau_{2}(2)}

\displaystyle\cdots\Upsilon_{2}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),k,\tau_{k}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),k,\tau_{k}(2)}\Upsilon_{k}]

Then, by Holder and the definition of
\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,1},\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,2}\in\R^{d}
, we can show that
F(\Upsilon_{1},...,\Upsilon_{k})
defines a multilinear functional on
L_{\infty}(S_{\infty}^{d})
.

By Corollary
6.6
, it suffices to prove the claim for
(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{k})
such that
(\frac{1}{\beta_{1}},...,\frac{1}{\beta_{k}})
are extreme points of the set
\{(x_{1},...,x_{k})\in[0,1]^{k}:\sum\limits_{\lambda=1}^{k}x_{\lambda}=1-\frac{k}{2q}\}
, because all the other
(\frac{1}{\beta_{1}},...,\frac{1}{\beta_{k}})
are convex combinations of the extreme points and the result follows from interpolation. Therefore, we only need to prove the claim for the extreme case when one of the
\beta_{\lambda}
’s is
\frac{q}{q-k}
and all the others are
\infty
. By symmetry, we only need to consider the case
\beta_{1}=\cdots=\beta_{k-1}=\infty
and
\beta_{k}=\frac{q}{q-k}
.

Let
\eta=(\eta(1),\eta(2))
be a uniformly distributed random vector in
[n]\times[pm]
such that
\eta
is independent with
\{\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in[k]}
,
S_{1},S_{2},G_{1},G_{2}
, and
\{\Upsilon_{1},...,\Upsilon_{k}\}
.

For all
\lambda\in[k]
, define random vectors
\Theta_{1,\lambda}=\Theta_{\eta,\lambda,1}
and
\Theta_{2,\lambda}=\Theta_{\eta,\lambda,2}
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\E[\tr\Theta_{(l,\gamma),1,\tau_{1}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),1,\tau_{1}(2)}\Upsilon_{1}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),2,\tau_{2}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),2,\tau_{2}(2)}

\displaystyle\cdots\Upsilon_{2}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),k,\tau_{k}(1)}^{T}\Theta_{(l,\gamma),k,\tau_{k}(2)}\Upsilon_{k}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle pmn\cdot\E[\tr\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}\Upsilon_{1}\Theta_{\tau_{2}(1),2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{2}(2),2}\cdots\Upsilon_{2}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(1),k}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(2),k}\Upsilon_{k}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\cdot\E[\tr\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}\Upsilon_{1}\Theta_{\tau_{2}(1),2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{2}(2),2}\cdots\Upsilon_{2}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(1),k}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(2),k}\Upsilon_{k}]

where
\mathcal{S}:=pmn
is the number of non-zero entries in
S
.
Essentially, we have written the sum over indices
(l,\gamma)
as an expectation over uniformly chosen
(l,\gamma)
times the number of tuples
(l,\gamma)
.

Let
\Upsilon_{k}=V_{k}|\Upsilon_{k}|
be the polar decomposition. By matrix Holder inequality (Lemma 5.3. in
[
13
]
), we have

\displaystyle|\E[\tr\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}\Upsilon_{1}\Theta_{\tau_{2}(1),2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{2}(2),2}\cdots\Upsilon_{k-1}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(1),k}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(2),k}\Upsilon_{k}]|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\E[\tr|\Upsilon_{k}|^{1/2}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}\Upsilon_{1}\Theta_{\tau_{2}(1),2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{2}(2),2}\cdots\Upsilon_{k-1}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(1),k}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(2),k}V_{k}|\Upsilon_{k}|^{1/2}]|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\E[\tr\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}\Upsilon_{1}\cdots\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(1),k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(2),k/2}\Upsilon_{k/2}

\displaystyle\cdot\Upsilon_{k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(2),k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(1),k/2}\cdots\Upsilon_{1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|\Upsilon_{k}|]|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\cdot|\E[\tr\Theta_{\tau_{k}(2),k}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(1),k}\Upsilon_{k-1}\cdots\Upsilon_{k/2+1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2+1}(2),k/2+1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2+1}(1),k/2+1}

\displaystyle\cdot\Theta_{\tau_{k/2+1}(1),k/2+1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2+1}(2),k/2+1}\Upsilon_{k/2+1}\cdots\Upsilon_{k-1}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(1),k}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(2),k}V_{k}|\Upsilon_{k}|V_{k}^{T}]|^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\text{factor 1})\cdot(\text{factor 2})

where, assuming
k
is even, in the notation of Proposition 5.1 in
[
13
]
, we set

\displaystyle Y_{1}=|\Upsilon_{k}|^{1/2}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}\Upsilon_{1}\cdots\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(1),k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(2),k/2}\Upsilon_{k/2}

and

\displaystyle Y_{2}=\Theta_{\tau_{k/2+1}(1),k/2+1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2+1}(2),k/2+1}\Upsilon_{k/2+1}\cdots\Upsilon_{k-1}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(1),k}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k}(2),k}V_{k}|\Upsilon_{k}|^{1/2}

with the exponents being
p_{1}=p_{2}=2
. When
k
is odd, the argument can be modified as suggested in Step 4 of the proof of Proposition 5.1 in
[
13
]
.

We focus on the factor

\displaystyle(\text{factor 1})=

\displaystyle|\E[\tr\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}\Upsilon_{1}\cdots\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(1),k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(2),k/2}\Upsilon_{k/2}

\displaystyle\cdot\Upsilon_{k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(2),k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(1),k/2}\cdots\Upsilon_{1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|\Upsilon_{k}|]|^{1/2}

and the analysis of the other factor will be similar.

Let

\displaystyle\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}=\Lambda_{1}|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|

be the polar decomposition.

Therefore, we have, for some
r\geq 1
to be fixed later,

(7.7)

\displaystyle(\text{factor 1})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\E[\tr\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}\Upsilon_{1}\cdots\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(1),k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(2),k/2}\Upsilon_{k/2}

\displaystyle\cdot\Upsilon_{k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(2),k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(1),k/2}\cdots\Upsilon_{1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|\Upsilon_{k}|]|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\E[\tr|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1-1/r}\Lambda_{1}^{T}\Upsilon_{1}\cdots\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(1),k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(2),k/2}\Upsilon_{k/2}

\displaystyle\cdot\Upsilon_{k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(2),k/2}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{k/2}(1),k/2}\cdots\Upsilon_{1}^{T}\Lambda_{1}|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1-1/r}

\displaystyle\cdot|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r}|\Upsilon_{k}||\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r}]|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}\rVert_{2q}^{2-2/r}\prod\limits_{\lambda=2}^{k/2}\lVert\Theta_{\tau_{\lambda}(1),\lambda}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{\lambda}(2),\lambda}\rVert_{2q}^{2}\prod\limits_{\lambda=1}^{k/2}{\lVert\Upsilon_{\lambda}\rVert_{\infty}^{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\lVert|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r}|\Upsilon_{k}||\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r}\rVert_{r}

where the last assertion follows from using the Hölder inequality with the exponents,

•

2q
for
\Theta_{\tau_{j}(1),j}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{j}(2),j}
and
\Theta_{\tau_{j}(2),j}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{j}(1),j}
for
j=2,\ldots,k/2
. Note that there are
2(\frac{k}{2}-1)=k-2
many such terms.

•

\infty
for
\Upsilon_{j}
or
\Upsilon_{j}^{T}
for
j=1,\ldots,k/2
,
\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{1}^{T}
.

•

\frac{2qr}{r-1}
for each term
|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1-1/r}
.

•

r
for the term
|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r}|\Upsilon_{k}||\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r}
.

Then, the condition on exponents for Hölder inequality determines the value of
r
,

\frac{k-2}{2q}+\frac{2(r-1)}{2rq}+\frac{1}{r}=1

giving,
r=\frac{2q-2}{2q-k}
.

Starting from here, we bound all the factors that appear in the final line of (
7.7
). First, the factor
\lVert\Theta_{\tau_{\lambda}(1),\lambda}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{\lambda}(2),\lambda}\rVert_{2q}=\lVert\Theta_{1,\lambda}^{T}\Theta_{2,\lambda}\rVert_{2q}
can be bounded directly by using the following lemma.

Lemma 7.10

(Product of Random Rows)
.

Let
\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,1},\Theta_{(l,\gamma),\lambda,2}\in\R^{d}
be as in Lemma
7.8
. Let
\eta=(\eta(1),\eta(2))
be a uniformly distributed random vector in
[n]\times[pm]
such that
\eta
is independent with
\{\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in[k]}
,
S_{1},S_{2},G_{1},G_{2}
. For all
\lambda\in[k]
, define random vectors
\Theta_{1,\lambda}=\Theta_{\eta,\lambda,1}
and
\Theta_{2,\lambda}=\Theta_{\eta,\lambda,2}
. Then there exists a constant
c_{\ref*{theta1theta2}}
such that, for any integer
q\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\Theta_{1,\lambda}^{T}\Theta_{2,\lambda}\rVert_{q}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{\ref*{theta1theta2}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}}}{\mathcal{S}^{\frac{1}{q}}}

where
\mathcal{S}=pmn
.

Proof.

Note that for each fixed
1\leq\lambda\leq k/2
,

\displaystyle\lVert\Theta_{1,\lambda}^{T}\Theta_{2,\lambda}\rVert_{q}

\displaystyle=\lVert\xi_{\eta,\lambda}\mathbf{u}_{\eta(1)}e_{\mu_{\eta,\lambda}}^{T}S_{2}(t)U\rVert_{q}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\E[\tr\lvert\xi_{\eta,\lambda}\mathbf{u}_{\eta(1)}e_{\mu_{\eta,\lambda}}^{T}S_{2}(t)U\rvert^{q}]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=\left(\E[\tr\lvert\mathbf{u}_{\eta(1)}e_{\mu_{\eta,\lambda}}^{T}S_{2}(t)U\rvert^{q}]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=\left(\E\left[\frac{1}{d}\lVert\mathbf{u}_{\eta(1)}\rVert^{q}\lVert e_{\mu_{\eta,\lambda}}^{T}S_{2}(t)U\rVert^{q}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

Conditioning on
\eta
, we take expectation of the second factor over
\mu_{\eta,\lambda}
and
S_{2}
,

\displaystyle\lVert\Theta_{1,\lambda}^{T}\Theta_{2,\lambda}\rVert_{q}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\E_{\eta}\left[\frac{1}{d}\lVert\mathbf{u}_{\eta(1)}\rVert^{q}\E_{\mu_{\eta,\lambda},S_{2}(t)}[\lVert e_{\mu_{\eta,\lambda}}^{T}S_{2}(t)U\rVert^{q}]\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

Note that, conditioned on
\eta
,
\mu_{\eta,\lambda}
is uniformly distributed over a subset of
[m]
. Therefore, by Lemma
6.2
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\Theta_{1,\lambda}^{T}\Theta_{2,\lambda}\rVert_{q}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\E_{\eta}\left[\frac{1}{d}\lVert\mathbf{u}_{\eta(1)}\rVert^{q}\E_{\mu_{\eta,\lambda},S_{2}(t)}[\lVert e_{\mu_{\eta,\lambda}}^{T}S_{2}(t)U\rVert^{q}]\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\ref*{lem:rownormbound}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}}\left(\E_{\eta}\left[\frac{1}{d}\lVert\mathbf{u}_{\eta(1)}\rVert^{q}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=c_{\ref*{lem:rownormbound}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}d}\sum_{(l,\gamma)\in[n]\times[pm]}\lVert u_{l}\rVert^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=c_{\ref*{lem:rownormbound}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}}\left(\frac{pm}{\mathcal{S}d}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\lVert u_{l}\rVert^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\ref*{lem:rownormbound}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}}\left(\frac{pm}{\mathcal{S}d}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\lVert u_{l}\rVert^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{\ref*{theta1theta2}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}}}{\mathcal{S}^{\frac{1}{q}}}

where in the last line we use the fact that
\sum_{l=1}^{n}\lVert u_{l}\rVert^{2}=d
.

∎

Next, we have the following bound for the factor
\lVert|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r}|\Upsilon_{k}||\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r}\rVert_{r}
.

\displaystyle\lVert|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r}|\Upsilon_{k}||\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r}\rVert_{r}^{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\tr((|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r}|\Upsilon_{k}||\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|^{1/r})^{r})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\E\tr(|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}||\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\tr(|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}|\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}||\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1}|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\tr(|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}(\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1})^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1})

where the inequality is due to the Lieb Thirring Inequality (
[
13
, Lemma 5.4]
).

Here, we have two possible cases. The first case (CASE I) is
\tau_{1}(1)=1
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle(\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1})^{T}(\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta_{1,1}^{T}\Theta_{2,1}(\Theta_{2,1}^{T}\Theta_{1,1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(Z_{\eta,1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{\eta,1}U

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\E\tr(|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}(\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1})^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\tr(|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}(Z_{\eta,1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{\eta,1}U)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\tr(|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}\E_{\eta}((Z_{\eta,1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{\eta,1}U))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\tr(|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}}\E\tr(|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}\E_{Z}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}}\lVert|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}\rVert_{\frac{2q}{2q-2}}\cdot\lVert\E_{Z}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)\rVert_{q}

Plugging this estimate and the previous estimate on
\lVert\Theta_{\tau_{j}(1),j}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{j}(2),j}\rVert_{q}
into inequality
7.7
, we have

(7.8)

\displaystyle(\text{factor 1})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\Theta_{2,1}^{T}\Theta_{1,1}\rVert_{2q}^{2-2/r}\prod\limits_{\lambda=2}^{k/2}\lVert\Theta_{1,\lambda}^{T}\Theta_{2,\lambda}\rVert_{2q}^{2}\prod\limits_{\lambda=1}^{k/2}{\lVert\Upsilon_{\lambda}\rVert_{\infty}^{2}}\lVert|\Theta_{2,1}^{T}\Theta_{1,1}|^{1/r}|\Upsilon_{k}||\Theta_{2,1}^{T}\Theta_{1,1}|^{1/r}\rVert_{r}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{c_{\ref*{theta1theta2}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}}}{\mathcal{S}^{\frac{1}{q}}}\right)^{2-2/r}\left(\frac{c_{\ref*{theta1theta2}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}}}{\mathcal{S}^{\frac{1}{q}}}\right)^{k-2}\prod\limits_{\lambda=1}^{k/2}{\lVert\Upsilon_{\lambda}\rVert_{\infty}^{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}}\lVert|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}\rVert_{\frac{2q}{2q-2}}\cdot\left\lVert\E_{Z}\left(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U\right)\right\rVert_{q}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{c_{\ref*{theta1theta2}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}}}{\mathcal{S}^{\frac{1}{q}}}\right)^{\frac{2qk-4q}{2q-2}}\prod\limits_{\lambda=1}^{k/2}{\lVert\Upsilon_{\lambda}\rVert_{\infty}^{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}}\lVert|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}\rVert_{\frac{2q}{2q-2}}\cdot\left\lVert\E_{Z}\left(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U\right)\right\rVert_{q}\right)^{1/r}

To bound the term
\lVert\E_{Z}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)\rVert_{q}
, we can use the following lemma to relate it to
\Gamma(t)
.

Lemma 7.11

(Replacement by the Original Matrix)
.

Let
S_{1}(t)
,
S_{2}(t)
,
\Gamma(t)
, and
Z_{(l,\gamma),\lambda}
be as in Lemma
7.8
.

We have

\displaystyle\lVert\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)\rVert_{q}^{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q})

and

\displaystyle\lVert\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)\rVert_{q}^{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q})

Proof.

Let
W_{(l,\gamma),1}
be the gaussian model for
Z_{(l,\gamma),1}
, i.e., the entries of
W_{(l,\gamma),1}
are gaussian and the covariance structure between entries of
W_{(l,\gamma),1}
is the same as
Z_{(l,\gamma),1}
, such that the family
\{W_{(l,\gamma),1}:(l,\gamma)\in\Xi\}\cup\{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}:(l,\gamma)\in\Xi\}
are mutually independent. Let
Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)=\sqrt{t}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}+\sqrt{1-t}W_{(l,\gamma),1}
. With this notation, we know that
\sum\limits_{(l,\gamma)\in\Xi}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)
has the same distribution with
S_{1}(t)
.

We observe that

\displaystyle\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)U)

because for fixed
(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}
, the value

\displaystyle\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)

only depends on the covariance structure of
Z_{(l,\gamma),1}
.

Also, we have

\displaystyle\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)U)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)}((\sum_{(l,\gamma)}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t))U)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U)

because the cross terms have zero expectation by independence.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}}\left(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U)

Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}}\left(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{1}(t)U)(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)

Now we look at
\E_{S_{1}(t)}(\Gamma(t)^{2})
. We have

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}(\Gamma(t)^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U)

\displaystyle+\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{1}(t)U)(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)

\displaystyle+\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)

\displaystyle+\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{1}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U)

To simplify the last two terms, we need the following observation. Consider a random matrix
X
whose entries have variance
1
and zero covariances. Let
Y
be a deterministic matrix. Then direct calculation can show that
\E_{X}XYX=Y^{T}
.

In fact, we have

\displaystyle(XY)_{i,\beta}=\sum_{\alpha}X_{i,\alpha}Y_{\alpha,\beta}

and therefore

\displaystyle\E_{X}(XYX)_{i,j}=\E_{X}(\sum_{\beta}\sum_{\alpha}X_{i,\alpha}Y_{\alpha,\beta}X_{\beta,j})=\sum_{\beta}\sum_{\alpha}\E_{X}(X_{i,\alpha}Y_{\alpha,\beta}X_{\beta,j})

We observe that
\E_{X}(X_{i,\alpha}Y_{\alpha,\beta}X_{\beta,j})=Y_{j,i}
if
\alpha=j
and
\beta=i
. When
(\alpha,\beta)\neq(j,i)
, we always have
\E_{X}(X_{i,\alpha}Y_{\alpha,\beta}X_{\beta,j})=0
. Therefore, we have
\E_{X}(XYX)_{i,j}=Y_{j,i}
, and then we conclude
\E_{X}(XYX)=Y^{T}
.

Observing that the matrix
(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}
has entries with variance
1
and zero covariances, the above analysis gives that

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}))(S_{2}(t)U)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)

and

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{1}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}(\Gamma(t)^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U)

\displaystyle+\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{1}(t)U)(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)

\displaystyle+\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)

\displaystyle+\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{1}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U)

\displaystyle+\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{1}(t)U)(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)

\displaystyle+2(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)

Since all the matrices in the sum

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U)

\displaystyle+\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{1}(t)U)(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)

\displaystyle+2(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)

are positively semidefinite, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U)\leq\E_{S_{1}(t)}(\Gamma(t)^{2})

and

\displaystyle\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(S_{1}(t)U)(S_{1}(t)U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)\leq\E_{S_{1}(t)}(\Gamma(t)^{2})

Therefore, by
[
41
, Theorem 2.10]
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)\rVert_{q}^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lVert\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U)\rVert_{q}^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\tr((\E_{S_{1}(t)}((S_{1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}S_{1}(t)U))^{q})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\E\tr((\E_{S_{1}(t)}(\Gamma(t)^{2}))^{q})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\E\tr((\Gamma(t)^{2})^{q})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q})

Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\lVert\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)Z_{(l,\gamma),1}U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)\rVert_{q}^{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q})

∎

Therefore, we have bounded all the factors in the last line of (
7.7
), and the calculations give

\displaystyle(\text{factor 1})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{c_{\ref*{theta1theta2}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}}}{\mathcal{S}^{\frac{1}{q}}}\right)^{\frac{2qk-4q}{2q-2}}\prod\limits_{\lambda=1}^{k/2}{\lVert\Upsilon_{\lambda}\rVert_{\infty}^{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot(\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}}\lVert|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}\rVert_{\frac{2q}{2q-2}}\cdot\lVert\E_{Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)}(\sum_{(l,\gamma)}(Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)U)^{T}(S_{2}(t)U)(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{(l,\gamma),1}(t)U)\rVert_{q})^{1/r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{-1}(c_{\ref*{theta1theta2}}(pm)^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\max\{pd,q\}})^{\frac{2qk-4q}{2q-2}}\prod\limits_{\lambda=1}^{k/2}{\lVert\Upsilon_{\lambda}\rVert_{\infty}^{2}}\cdot\lVert\Upsilon_{k}\rVert_{\frac{2q}{2q-k}}(\E\tr((\Gamma(t))^{2q}))^{(1/q)(1/r)}

The second case (CASE II) is
\tau_{1}(1)=2
, or
\tau_{1}(2)=1
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle(\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1})^{T}(\Theta_{\tau_{1}(2),1}^{T}\Theta_{\tau_{1}(1),1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Theta_{1,1}^{T}\Theta_{2,1})^{T}(\Theta_{1,1}^{T}\Theta_{2,1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{\eta,1}U(Z_{\eta,1}U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\lVert|\Theta_{1,1}^{T}\Theta_{2,1}|^{1/r}|\Upsilon_{k}||\Theta_{1,1}^{T}\Theta_{2,1}|^{1/r}\rVert_{r^{\prime}}^{r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\E\tr(|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{\eta,1}U(Z_{\eta,1}U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)

Following similar calculations as in CASE (I), we obtain

\displaystyle\E\tr(|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{\eta,1}U(Z_{\eta,1}U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\tr(|\Upsilon_{k}|^{r}\E_{\eta}(S_{2}(t)U)^{T}Z_{\eta,1}U(Z_{\eta,1}U)^{T}S_{2}(t)U)
